u^{0}_{1,3}=\sqrt{T^{0}}(1+\rho^{0}\sqrt{T^{0}})
given in (
1.50
), and
(f_{1},f_{2},g)(t,\bar{x},\zeta)
are the known source functions. By the properties of compressible Euler system, we can assume the viscosity and heat conductivity, respectively,

(2.7)

\displaystyle\mu(T^{0})\geq\mu_{0}>0\,,\ \tfrac{3}{5}\kappa(T^{0})\geq\kappa_{0}>0

for some constants
\mu_{0},\kappa_{0}>0
. The system (
2.6
) should be imposed on the non-homogeneous Robin boundary conditions, namely,

(2.8)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \big(\partial_{\zeta}u_{i}-R_{u}u_{i}\big)\big|_{\zeta=0}=b_{i}(t,\bar{x})\,,\ \big(\partial_{\zeta}\theta-R_{\theta}\theta\big)\big|_{\zeta=0}=a(t,\bar{x})\,,\\
\lim_{\zeta\to\infty}(u,\theta)(t,\bar{x},\zeta)=0\,,\end{aligned}\right.

where the known functions
(R_{u},R_{\theta})=(R_{u},R_{\theta})(\rho^{0},u^{0},T^{0})
, and

(2.9)

\displaystyle R_{u}\geq R^{0}_{u}>0\,,R_{\theta}\geq R^{0}_{\theta}>0

for some constants
R_{u}^{0}
and
R_{\theta}^{0}
. We impose (
2.6
) with initial data

(2.10)

\displaystyle u(t,\bar{x},\zeta)|_{t=0}=u_{0}(\bar{x},\zeta)\,,\ \theta(t,\bar{x},\zeta)|_{t=0}=\theta_{0}(\bar{x},\zeta)\,,

which satisfy the corresponding compatibility conditions. We remark that the
\theta
-equation in (
2.6
)-(
2.10
) is independent of
u
-equation. As a result, we can first solve
\theta
, and then solve
u
, where
\theta
involved in
u
-equation can be regarded as the known source function.

Recalling the definitions of
\mathbb{H}^{k}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
and
\mathbb{H}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
in (
1.67
) and (
1.68
), we give the following results.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
k\geq 3
,
l\geq 0
, and the compatibility conditions for the initial data (
2.10
) be satisfied. Assume

(2.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{1}:=\|u_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\big(\|b(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k+3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|f(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k+1}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}\big)

\displaystyle+\|\theta_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}+\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\big(\|a(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k+3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|g(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k+1}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}\big)<\infty\,.

Then there exists a unique smooth solution
(u,\theta)(t,\bar{x},\zeta)
to (
2.6
)-(
2.10
) over
t\in[0,\tau]
satisfying

(2.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\Big(\|(u,\theta)

\displaystyle(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}+\|B_{c}(u,\theta)(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{\zeta}(u,\theta)(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k}_{l}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}+\|B_{c}(u,\theta)(s)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq C(\tau,E_{k+1})\mathbb{E}_{1}\,,

where
B_{c}(u,\theta)=(u,\theta)|_{\zeta=0}-(\tfrac{1}{R_{u}}b,\tfrac{1}{R_{\theta}}a)
.

The proof of Lemma
2.2
will be given in Section
5
.

We then introduce a result on the existence of solutions of the Knudsen boundary layer problem. Consider the following half-space linear equation for
f(t,\bar{x},\xi,v)
over
(t,\bar{x},\xi,v)\in[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
:

(2.13)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{\xi}f+\mathcal{L}^{0}f=S(t,\bar{x},\xi,v)\,,\\
&f(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},v_{3})|_{v_{3}>0}=f(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})+\mathbbm{f}_{k}(t,\bar{x},\bar{v},-v_{3})\,,\\
&\lim_{\xi\to\infty}f(t,\bar{x},\xi,v)=0\,.\end{aligned}\right.

For the above equation, Golse, Perthame and Sulem
[
15
]
has proved an existence result in the norm
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle v\rangle e^{2\eta\xi}f^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|e^{\eta\xi}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{\xi}}\mathrm{d}v
. Due to the higher order derivatives are required, Guo, Huang and Wang
[
23
]
gave a more smooth version of existence result, hence the following lemma.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
0<a<\frac{1}{2}
,
r\geq 0
and
l\geq 3
. For each
(t,\bar{x})\in[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we assume that
S\in(\mathcal{N}^{0})^{\bot}
,
\mathbbm{f}_{k}(t,\bar{x},v)
satisfies the solvability condition (
1.44
), and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{2}:=\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\!\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\Big\{\|\langle v\rangle^{l}(\mathfrak{M}^{0})^{-a}\partial_{t,\bar{x}}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\mathbbm{f}_{k}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\bar{x},v}\cap L^{2}_{\bar{x}}L^{\infty}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\|\langle v\rangle^{l}(\mathfrak{M}^{0})^{-a}e^{\lambda_{0}\xi}\partial_{t,\bar{x}}^{\alpha}S(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\bar{x},\xi,v}\cap L^{2}_{\bar{x}}L^{\infty}_{\xi,v}}\Big\}<+\infty

for some positive constant
\lambda_{0}>0
. Then the Knudsen boundary layer equation (
2.13
) has a unique solution
f(t,\bar{x},\xi,v)
satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\Big\{\|\langle v\rangle^{l}(\mathfrak{M}^{0})^{-a}\partial_{t,\bar{x}}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle f(t,\cdot,0,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\bar{x},v}\cap L^{2}_{\bar{x}}L^{\infty}_{v}}

(2.14)

\displaystyle+\|\langle v\rangle^{l}(\mathfrak{M}^{0})^{-a}e^{\lambda\xi}\partial_{t,\bar{x}}^{\alpha}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\bar{x},\xi,v}\cap L^{2}_{\bar{x}}L^{\infty}_{\xi,v}}\Big\}\leq\tfrac{C}{\lambda_{0}-\lambda}\mathbb{E}_{2}

for all
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0})
, where
C>0
is independence of
(t,\bar{x})
. Moreover, if
S
is in
C\big([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\big)
and
\mathbbm{f}_{k}(t,\bar{x},\bar{v},-v_{3})
is in
C\big([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\big)
, then the solution
f(t,\bar{x},\xi,v)
is continuous away from the grazing set
[0,\tau]\times\Sigma_{0}
.

We remark that it is hard to obtain the normal derivatives estimates for the boundary value problem (
2.13
). Lemma
2.3
merely give the tangential and time derivatives estimates.

2.2.
Control the expansions
F_{k}
,
F_{k}^{b}
and
F_{k}^{bb}
(
1\leq k\leq 5
)

Based on results in Lemma
2.1
, Lemma
2.2
and
2.3
, following the analogously arguments in Proposition 5.1 of
[
23
]
, we obtain the uniform bounds of the expansions
F_{k}
,
F_{k}^{b}
and
F_{k}^{bb}
(
1\leq k\leq 5
) as follows. For simplicity, we omit the details of proof here.

Proposition 2.1
.

Let
0<\tfrac{1}{2z}(1-z)<a<\frac{1}{2}
, where
z\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
is given in (
1.62
), and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be in Proposition
1.1
. There are
s_{k},s_{k}^{b},s_{k}^{bb},l_{k}^{b}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p_{k},p_{k}^{b},p_{k}^{bb}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}

(1\leq k\leq 5)
satisfying

\displaystyle s_{0}\geq s_{1}+10\,,\ s_{1}=s_{1}^{b}=s_{1}^{bb}\,,\ s_{k-1}^{bb}\gg s_{k}\gg s_{k}^{b}\gg s_{k}^{bb}\gg 1\ (2\leq k\leq 5)\,,

\displaystyle p_{k}\gg p_{k}^{b}\gg p_{k}^{bb}\gg p_{k+1}\gg 1\ (1\leq k\leq 4)\,,

and
l_{j}^{k}=l_{k}^{b}+2(s_{k}^{b}-j)

(0\leq j\leq s_{k}^{b},1\leq k\leq 5)
with

\displaystyle l^{5}_{j}\geq 8\,,\ l_{j}^{k}\geq 2l_{j}^{k+1}+26\ (1\leq k\leq 4)\,,

such that if the initial data
(\rho_{k}^{in},u_{k}^{in},\theta_{k}^{in})

(1\leq k\leq 3)
in (
1.18
) and
(\bar{u}^{b,in}_{k},\theta_{k}^{b,in})

(1\leq k\leq 3)
in (
1.34
) satisfy (
1.72
), i.e.,
\mathcal{E}^{in}<\infty
, then there are solutions
F_{k}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{k}
,
F^{b}_{k}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}f^{b}_{k}
and
F_{k}^{bb}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}f_{k}^{bb}

(1\leq k\leq 5)
constructed in Subsection
1.3.5
over the time interval
t\in[0,\tau]
subjecting to the uniform bounds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\sum_{k=1}^{5}\Big\{

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma+|\beta|\leq s_{k}}\|\langle v\rangle^{p_{k}}\mathfrak{M}^{-a}\partial_{t}^{\gamma}\partial_{x}^{\beta}f_{k}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{s_{k}^{b}}\sum_{2\gamma+|\bar{\beta}|=j}\|\langle v\rangle^{p_{k}^{b}}(\mathfrak{M}^{0})^{-a}\partial_{t}^{\gamma}\partial_{\bar{x}}^{\bar{\beta}}f^{b}_{k}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{l^{k}_{j}}L^{\infty}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\gamma+|\bar{\beta}|\leq s_{k}^{bb}}\|e^{\frac{\xi}{2^{k-1}}}\langle v\rangle^{p_{k}^{bb}}(\mathfrak{M}^{0})^{-a}\partial_{t}^{\gamma}\partial_{\bar{x}}^{\bar{\beta}}f_{k}^{bb}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\bar{x},\xi,v}\cap L^{2}_{\bar{x}}L^{\infty}_{\xi,v}}\Big\}

(2.15)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\tau,\|(\rho^{in}-\rho_{\#},\mathfrak{u}^{in},T^{in}-T_{\#})\|_{H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})}+\mathcal{E}^{in}\Big)\,.

We remark that, for
1\leq k\leq 5
, the viscous boundary layers
F_{k}^{b}
decay algebraically associated with
\zeta>0
, and the Knudsen boundary layers
F_{k}^{bb}
decay exponentially associated with
\xi>0
. These suffice to dominate the quantities
R_{\varepsilon}^{b}
and
R_{\varepsilon}^{bb}
(in (
1.56
) and (
1.3.5
), respectively) while deriving the uniform
L^{2}
-
L^{\infty}
bounds for the remainder
F_{R,\varepsilon}
.

3.
Uniform bounds for remainder
F_{R,\varepsilon}
: Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section, we aim at proving our main theorem by applying the
L^{2}
-
L^{\infty}
arguments
[
20
,
21
]
, which is sufficient to estimate
\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2}
and
\|h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\infty}
associated with the remainder
F_{R,\varepsilon}
in (
1.52
). Here
f_{R,\varepsilon}
and
h_{R,\varepsilon}^{\ell}
are defined in (
1.59
). The proof relies on an interplay between
L^{2}
and
L^{\infty}
estimates for the Boltzmann equation. The
L^{2}
norm of
f_{R,\varepsilon}
is controlled by the
L^{\infty}
norm of the high-velocity part and vice versa.

Note that the remainder equation (
1.52
) with Maxwell-type reflection boundary condition contains the coefficients
F_{k}
,
F_{k}^{b}
,
F_{k}^{bb}
(
1\leq k\leq 5
) and
R_{\varepsilon}
,
R_{\varepsilon}^{b}
,
R_{\varepsilon}^{bb}
composed of
F_{k}
,
F_{k}^{b}
,
F_{k}^{bb}
(
1\leq k\leq 5
). Thanks to Proposition
2.1
, following the similar arguments in Section 6 of
[
23
]
, we can bound the all norms associated with them appeared in estimates of
L^{2}
-
L^{\infty}
arguments by some constants independent of
\varepsilon
. For simplicity of presentation, the corresponding quantities will be directly bounded by a constant
C>0
without details in what follows.

We now state the following two key lemmas.

Lemma 3.1

(
L^{2}
Estimates)
.

Under the same assumptions in Proposition
2.1
and
\ell\geq 9-2\gamma_{0}
, let
(\rho,\mathfrak{u},T)
be a smooth solution to the Euler equations over
t\in[0,\tau]
obtained in Proposition
1.1
. Let
c_{0}>0
be mentioned in (
1.13
), and the local Maxwellian
M_{w}(t,\bar{x},v)
of the boundary be assumed as in (
1.71
). Then there are constants
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
and
C=C\big(\mathfrak{M},F_{k},F^{b}_{k},F^{bb}_{k};1\leq k\leq 5\big)>0
such that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+\tfrac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P})f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\nu}^{2}+c_{1}\iint_{\Sigma_{-}}|v\cdot n(x)||L\gamma_{+}f_{R,\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{\Sigma_{-}}

(3.1)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+\varepsilon^{2}\|h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\infty}\big)\big(\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}\big)+C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}

over
t\in[0,\tau]
, where
c_{1}=1-3\rho_{\#}\sqrt{3T_{\#}}>0
and
\rho_{\#},T_{\#}>0
are given in Proposition
1.1
.

Lemma 3.2

(
L^{\infty}
Estimate)
.

Under the same assumptions in Lemma
3.1
, there are constants
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}>0
and
C=C\big(\mathfrak{M},F_{k},F^{b}_{k},F^{bb}_{k};1\leq k\leq 5\big)>0
such that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
,

(3.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}(s)\|_{\infty}\leq C\Big(\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{\infty}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(s)\|_{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}\Big)\,.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Based on Lemma
3.1
and (
3.2
), one has

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+\tfrac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P})f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\nu}^{2}+c_{1}\iint_{\Sigma_{-}}|v\cdot n(x)||L\gamma_{+}f_{R,\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\sigma_{\Sigma_{-}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big[1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}(\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{\infty}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(s)\|_{2}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2})\big](\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{2}+\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2})+C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}\,.

The Grönwall inequality yields

\displaystyle\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2}^{2}+1\leq(\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+1)e^{Ct\big\{1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}(\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}\|h_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{\infty}+\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2})\big\}}\,.

For bounded
\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2}
and small
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}\})
, utilizing the Taylor expansion of the exponential function in the above inequality, one has

\displaystyle\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2}^{2}+1\leq C_{1}(\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+1)\big[1+\sqrt{\varepsilon}(\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}\|h_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{\infty}+\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2})\big]\,.

For
t\leq\tau
, by using the Young inequality, we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.1
by the bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t)\|_{2}\leq C_{\tau}\big(1+\|f_{R,\varepsilon}(0)\|_{2}+\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}h_{R,\varepsilon}^{\ell}(0)\|_{\infty}\big)\,.

∎

3.1.
L^{2}
-estimate: Proof of Lemma
3.1

From (
1.52
) and (
1.59
), we see that
f_{R,\varepsilon}
satisfies

(3.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{R,\varepsilon}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{R,\varepsilon}+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}Lf_{R,\varepsilon}=-\tfrac{\{\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\}\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}f_{R,\varepsilon}+\tfrac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}B(\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon},\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{5}\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{k-2}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}\Big\{B(F_{k}+F_{k}^{b}+F_{k}^{bb},\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon})+B(\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon},F_{k}+F_{k}^{b}+F_{k}^{bb})\Big\}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}(R_{\varepsilon}+R_{\varepsilon}^{b}+R_{\varepsilon}^{bb})

with boundary condition

(3.4)

\displaystyle f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},v_{3})|_{v_{3}>0}=f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}\,,

where

(3.5)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}}\Gamma_{\varepsilon}-\tfrac{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\Big\{

\displaystyle f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2\pi}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\Big\}\,.

Here
\Gamma_{\varepsilon}
is given in (
1.54
).

Multiplying the equation (
3.3
) by
f_{R,\varepsilon}
, integrating the resultant equation over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and using (
1.13
), one obtains

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+\tfrac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P})f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\nu}^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}|f_{R,\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}B(\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon},\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon})f_{R,\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{5}\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{k-2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}\big\{B(F_{k}+F^{b}_{k}+F^{bb}_{k},\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+B(\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}f_{R,\varepsilon},F_{k}+F^{b}_{k}+F^{bb}_{k})\big\}f_{R,\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

(3.6)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}}}\big(R_{\varepsilon}+R^{b}_{\varepsilon}+R^{bb}_{\varepsilon}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,.

Following the similar arguments in Section 2.1 of
[
22
]
,

(3.7)

\displaystyle\text{RHS of \eqref{Remiander_Eq-L2-esimate-1}}\leq C_{\lambda}\varepsilon^{2}\|h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\infty}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}+C\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}+C\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+\tfrac{C\lambda^{3-\gamma_{0}}}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P})f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\nu}^{2}

for some small
\lambda>0
to be determined.

We next focus on estimating the boundary integral term
-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\bar{x}
. Recalling the boundary condition (
3.4
), one has

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big\{\int_{-\infty}^{0}+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big\}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})+\sqrt{\varepsilon}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

(3.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sqrt{\varepsilon}\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\tfrac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2}\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}}_{I_{2}}\,.

By using the definition of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}
in (
3.5
), one has

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle-\alpha_{\varepsilon}\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{2\pi}\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})\cdot\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}^{\prime}f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3}^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}^{\prime}\Big)\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

(3.9)

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})\Gamma_{\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}=:I_{11}+I_{12}+I_{13}\,.

Obviously, the term
I_{11}
is negative, thus it is a good term in the
L^{2}
-estimates as a boundary decay rate. We then control the quantity
I_{12}
. Recalling the assumption (
1.71
) of the local Maxwellian
M_{w}(t,\bar{x},v)
of the boundary, we derive from direct calculation that

\displaystyle I_{12}=\sqrt{2\pi}\alpha_{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}\,.

By Hölder inequality and the fact
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}\mathfrak{M}^{0}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}=\frac{\rho^{0}\sqrt{T^{0}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}
, we further infer that

\displaystyle|I_{12}|\leq

\displaystyle\alpha_{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho^{0}\sqrt{T^{0}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\Big)\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\rho^{0}\sqrt{T^{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\cdot\alpha_{\varepsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

(3.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-3\sqrt{3}\rho_{\#}\sqrt{T_{\#}}I_{11}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from the Proposition
1.1
. Here the constant
3\sqrt{3}\rho_{\#}\sqrt{T_{\#}}\in(0,1)
and the symbol
I_{11}
is given in (
3.1
).

Moreover, it is deduced from the Hölder inequality and Proposition
2.1
that

\displaystyle|I_{13}|\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}\alpha_{\varepsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

(3.11)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|\Gamma_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}\leq-\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}I_{11}+C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}\,.

Together with the bounds (
3.1
), (
3.1
) and (
3.1
), one sees that

(3.12)

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\big(c_{1}-\varepsilon\big)I_{11}+C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}\,.

where
c_{1}=1-3\sqrt{3}\rho_{\#}\sqrt{T_{\#}}>0

Similarly in estimating of (
3.12
), the quantity
|I_{2}|
can be bounded by

(3.13)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq-C_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\varepsilon}I_{11}+C_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}

for some constant
C_{\Gamma}>0
. We consequently derive from substituting (
3.12
) and (
3.13
) into (
3.1
) that

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\bar{x}=-(I_{1}+I_{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\big(c_{1}-\varepsilon-C_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\big)\alpha_{\varepsilon}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}-C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{3}-C_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}\,,

We then choose

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}=c_{1}\min\big\{\tfrac{1}{8},\tfrac{1}{8C_{\Gamma}}\big\}>0\,.

Therefore, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

(3.14)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}c_{1}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}-C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}

for some constant
C>0
. Together with (
3.1
) and (
3.7
), we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+\tfrac{c_{0}-C\lambda^{3-\gamma_{0}}}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P})f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\nu}^{2}

(3.15)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{1}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}v_{3}|f_{R,\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x},0,\bar{v},-v_{3})|^{2}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\mathrm{d}\bar{x}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\lambda}\varepsilon^{2}\|h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{\infty}\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}+C\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}+C\|f_{R,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}+C\sqrt{\varepsilon}^{2}

for some small
\lambda>0
to be determined. We further choose small
\lambda>0
such that
c_{0}-C\lambda^{3-\gamma_{0}}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}c_{0}>0
, and then finish the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

3.2.
L^{\infty}
estimate for
h^{\ell}_{R,\varepsilon}
: Proof of Lemma
3.2

As in
[
20
]
or
[
23
]
, we introduce the following backward
b
-characteristics of Boltzmann equation. Given
(t,x,v)
, let
[X(s),V(s)]
be determined by

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}X(s)=V(s)\,,\ \tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}V(s)=0\,,\\
&[X(t),V(t)]=[x,v]\,.\end{aligned}\right.

The solution is then given by

\displaystyle[X(s),V(s)]=[X(s;t,x,v),V(s;t,x,v)]=[x-(t-s)v,v]\,.

For any fixed
(x,v)
with
x\in\overline{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{+}
and
v_{3}\neq 0
, we define its backward exit time
t_{b}(x,v)\geq 0
to be the last moment at which the back-time straight line
[X(s;0,x,v),V(s;0,x,v)]
remains in
x\in\overline{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{+}
. Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle t_{b}(x,v)=\sup\big\{\tau_{0}\geq 0:x-\tau_{0}v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\big\}\,,

which means
x-t_{b}v\in\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
, i.e.,
x_{3}-t_{b}v_{3}=0
. We also define

\displaystyle x_{b}(x,v)=x(t_{b})=x-t_{b}v\in\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\,.

In the half space, for the case
v_{3}<0
, the back-time trajectory is a straight line and does not hit the boundary, see Figure
2
. For the case
v_{3}>0
, the back-time cycle will hit the boundary for one time, see Figure
1
.

\partial\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
n
v

\alpha_{\varepsilon}

:

:
